The company develops and supports the Integrated Care EHR (ICE), a cloud-based electronic health record system built on the open-source OpenEMR platform. The company appears to be closely tied to MyCHN (Community Health Network), a Federally Qualified Health Center (FQHC) operating 19 locations across the greater Houston and Gulf Coast areas (Brazoria, Galveston, and Harris County). The ICE login portal is hosted at ic-ehr.mychn.org, and the product's feature set is heavily oriented toward FQHC workflows — including UDS reporting, FQHC facility billing, and social determinants of health screening. The contact name on the CHPL listing (Dennis Shelton) appears to have a LinkedIn profile linking him to Community Health Network.\n     9→\n    10→CHN Tech Solutions appears to be a very small vendor, possibly a single-organization developer that built its EHR primarily for use at MyCHN and potentially offers it to other community health centers. There are no third-party reviews on G2, Capterra, or Software Advice, no press releases or media coverage, and no indication of a broader customer base beyond MyCHN. The vendor website is functional but sparse, typical of a small or internal-use health IT developer.\n    11→\n    12→## Product: Integrated Care EHR (ICE)\n    13→\n    14→CHPL ID: 11067\n    15→\n    16→### What It Is\n    17→\n    18→Integrated Care EHR (ICE) is a cloud-based EHR system built on top of OpenEMR, the popular open-source electronic health record platform. The login page at ic-ehr.mychn.org explicitly references OEMR (the nonprofit behind OpenEMR) and encourages installations to \"Register your installation with OEMR to receive important notifications, such as security fixes and new release announcements.\" The product is ONC-certified (version 3, certified 2022-12-13) and is designed for ambulatory primary care settings, particularly FQHCs.\n    19→\n    20→The certified module covers a moderate range of criteria: clinical data (CPOE for lab, demographics, clinical decision support, implantable device list, social determinants of health), transitions of care, clinical quality measures, patient health information capture, FHIR API access, and direct messaging. Notably absent from certification are: patient portal view-download-transmit (e)(1), e-prescribing criteria, and all public health reporting criteria (f)(1)–(f)(7) — though the vendor website describes e-prescribing and immunization reporting as product features.\n    21→\n    22→The product is cloud-hosted (compatible with AWS) and requires no on-premise hardware.\n    23→\n    24→### Users & Market\n    25→\n    26→The primary (and possibly only significant) deployment is at MyCHN (Community Health Network), a nonprofit FQHC in the greater Houston area. MyCHN operates 19 locations and provides:\n    27→- Primary care for adults and children\n    28→- Women's health / OBGYN (including high-risk pregnancy)\n    29→- Pediatrics\n    30→- Behavioral health (psychiatry and counseling, in-person and virtual)\n    31→- Dental services\n    32→- Pharmacy services\n    33→\n    34→The intended users per the CHPL listing are \"Physicians, Nurses, Medical Assistants, Health Care Administrators and Staff.\" The login page offers English and Spanish language options, consistent with serving a diverse Houston-area patient population.\n    35→\n    36→There is no evidence of other customers. The product does not appear on any major EHR review or comparison sites. No customer count, case studies, or testimonials are available beyond the MyCHN deployment.\n    37→\n    38→### Modules & Functionality\n    39→\n    40→Based on the vendor website (chntechsolutions.com/integrated-health-ehr-ice/) and the underlying OpenEMR platform:\n    41→\n    42→**Clinical Documentation**\n    43→- SOAP note-based documentation module\n    44→- Specialty modules for: Primary Care, Pediatrics, Women's Health, Psychiatry, Therapy, and Medication Assisted Therapy (MAT)\n    45→- Disaster response module with simplified intake documentation\n    46→- Chronic care management tracking, reporting, and billing\n    47→\n    48→**Screening & Assessment Tools**\n    49→- Integrated screening tools for: Social Determinants of Health (SDOH), Drug/Alcohol Abuse, Dental Care, Fall Risk, Human Trafficking, Lead Poisoning, Vision, TB, Smoking/Tobacco, Depression, and Zika Virus\n    50→- The vendor also offers a separate \"SDOH Platform\" and \"Elevations\" product for community-level social care coordination and closed-loop referrals\n    51→\n    52→**Patient Engagement**\n    53→- Patient portal with secure messaging\n    54→- iOS and Android mobile applications\n    55→- Bidirectional SMS/text messaging\n    56→- Forms integration\n    57→\n    58→**Billing & Financial**\n    59→- Specialized FQHC facility billing\n    60→- Traditional fee-for-service billing (customizable)\n    61→- Financial reports: payments collected by user, patient ledger, payor aging report, encounter detail with charges and collections\n    62→\n    63→**E-Prescribing**\n    64→- Electronic prescribing via Allscripts ePrescribe (now VeraDigm); the login page notes VeraDigm was discontinued May 2024 and the system transitioned to NewCrop (via SureScripts)\n    65→\n    66→**Lab & Imaging Interfaces**\n    67→- Interfaces with over 20 laboratories\n    68→- Radiology connectivity\n    69→\n    70→**Interoperability**\n    71→- Health Information Exchange (HIE) capability\n    72→- Immunization reporting\n    73→- Bidirectional interfaces where supported\n    74→- FHIR API (documented at onc.chntechsolutions.com)\n    75→- Direct messaging (h)(1) certified\n    76→- CCD operations via FHIR\n    77→\n    78→**Reporting**\n    79→- UDS (Uniform Data System) data collection and reporting — critical for FQHC compliance\n    80→- Meaningful Use / Promoting Interoperability reporting\n    81→- Patient-Centered Medical Home (PCMH) reporting\n    82→- Practice operational reports: cycle times, appointments/encounters by hour, patient no-show rates\n    83→\n    84→**Scheduling**\n    85→- Inherited from OpenEMR base platform (patient scheduling is a core OpenEMR feature)\n    86→- Appointment tracking and reporting\n    87→\n    88→**Infrastructure**\n    89→- Cloud-based (AWS-compatible), no on-premise hardware required\n    90→- Nightly maintenance window (11:30 PM – 1:00 AM per login page)\n    91→\n    92→### Data & Content\n    93→\n    94→Based on the features described above and the OpenEMR foundation, ICE stores:\n    95→\n    96→- **Clinical records**: SOAP notes, encounter documentation across multiple specialties (primary care, pediatrics, women's health, psychiatry, therapy, MAT)\n    97→- **Patient demographics**: Including SDOH data (certified for (a)(5) and (a)(15))\n    98→- **Screening/assessment data**: Extensive screening tool results for SDOH, substance abuse, depression, fall risk, human trafficking, lead poisoning, vision, TB, smoking, dental, and Zika\n    99→- **Medication data**: Prescriptions and e-prescribing records (historically via VeraDigm/Allscripts, now via NewCrop/SureScripts)\n   100→- **Lab results**: From 20+ interfaced laboratories\n   101→- **Radiology/imaging orders and results**\n   102→- **Implantable device data**: Certified for (a)(14)\n   103→- **Billing/claims data**: FQHC facility billing, fee-for-service claims, payment records, patient ledgers, payor aging\n   104→- **Scheduling data**: Appointments, encounter tracking, no-show tracking\n   105→- **Patient portal communications**: Secure messages between patients and providers\n   106→- **Transitions of care documents**: CCDs, certified for (b)(1)\n   107→- **Clinical quality measure data**: Certified for (c)(1)\n   108→- **Chronic care management records**: services. The company develops and supports the Integrated Care EHR (ICE), a cloud-based electronic health record system built on the open-source OpenEMR platform. The company appears to be closely tied to MyCHN (Community Health Network), a Federally Qualified Health Center (FQHC) operating 19 locations across the greater Houston and Gulf Coast areas (Brazoria, Galveston, and Harris County). The ICE login portal is hosted at ic-ehr.mychn.org, and the product's feature set is heavily oriented toward FQHC workflows — including UDS reporting, FQHC facility billing, and social determinants of health screening. The contact name on the CHPL listing (Dennis Shelton) appears to have a LinkedIn profile linking him to Community Health Network.\n\nCHN Tech Solutions appears to be a very small vendor, possibly a single-organization developer that built its EHR primarily for use at MyCHN and potentially offers it to other community health centers. There are no third-party reviews on G2, Capterra, or Software Advice, no press releases or media coverage, and no indication of a broader customer base beyond MyCHN. The vendor website is functional but sparse, typical of a small or internal-use health IT developer.\n\n## Product: Integrated Care EHR (ICE)\n\nCHPL ID: 11067\n\n### What It Is\n\nIntegrated Care EHR (ICE) is a cloud-based EHR system built on top of OpenEMR, the popular open-source electronic health record platform. The login page at ic-ehr.mychn.org explicitly references OEMR (the nonprofit behind OpenEMR) and encourages installations to \"Register your installation with OEMR to receive important notifications, such as security fixes and new release announcements.\" The product is ONC-certified (version 3, certified 2022-12-13) and is designed for ambulatory primary care settings, particularly FQHCs.\n\nThe certified module covers a moderate range of criteria: clinical data (CPOE for lab, demographics, clinical decision support, implantable device list, social determinants of health), transitions of care, clinical quality measures, patient health information capture, FHIR API access, and direct messaging. Notably absent from certification are: patient portal view-download-transmit (e)(1), e-prescribing criteria, and all public health reporting criteria (f)(1)–(f)(7) — though the vendor website describes e-prescribing and immunization reporting as product features.\n\nThe product is cloud-hosted (compatible with AWS) and requires no on-premise hardware.\n\n### Users & Market\n\nThe primary (and possibly only significant) deployment is at MyCHN (Community Health Network), a nonprofit FQHC in the greater Houston area. MyCHN operates 19 locations and provides:\n- Primary care for adults and children\n- Women's health / OBGYN (including high-risk pregnancy)\n- Pediatrics\n- Behavioral health (psychiatry and counseling, in-person and virtual)\n- Dental services\n- Pharmacy services\n\nThe intended users per the CHPL listing are \"Physicians, Nurses, Medical Assistants, Health Care Administrators and Staff.\" The login page offers English and Spanish language options, consistent with serving a diverse Houston-area patient population.\n\nThere is no evidence of other customers. The product does not appear on any major EHR review or comparison sites. No customer count, case studies, or testimonials are available beyond the MyCHN deployment.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nBased on the vendor website (chntechsolutions.com/integrated-health-ehr-ice/) and the underlying OpenEMR platform:\n\n**Clinical Documentation**\n- SOAP note-based documentation module\n- Specialty modules for: Primary Care, Pediatrics, Women's Health, Psychiatry, Therapy, and Medication Assisted Therapy (MAT)\n- Disaster response module with simplified intake documentation\n- Chronic care management tracking, reporting, and billing\n\n**Screening & Assessment Tools**\n- Integrated screening tools for: Social Determinants of Health (SDOH), Drug/Alcohol Abuse, Dental Care, Fall Risk, Human Trafficking, Lead Poisoning, Vision, TB, Smoking/Tobacco, Depression, and Zika Virus\n- The vendor also offers a separate \"SDOH Platform\" and \"Elevations\" product for community-level social care coordination and closed-loop referrals\n\n**Patient Engagement**\n- Patient portal with secure messaging\n- iOS and Android mobile applications\n- Bidirectional SMS/text messaging\n- Forms integration\n\n**Billing & Financial**\n- Specialized FQHC facility billing\n- Traditional fee-for-service billing (customizable)\n- Financial reports: payments collected by user, patient ledger, payor aging report, encounter detail with charges and collections\n\n**E-Prescribing**\n- Electronic prescribing via Allscripts ePrescribe (now VeraDigm); the login page notes VeraDigm was discontinued May 2024 and the system transitioned to NewCrop (via SureScripts)\n\n**Lab & Imaging Interfaces**\n- Interfaces with over 20 laboratories\n- Radiology connectivity\n\n**Interoperability**\n- Health Information Exchange (HIE) capability\n- Immunization reporting\n- Bidirectional interfaces where supported\n- FHIR API (documented at onc.chntechsolutions.com)\n- Direct messaging (h)(1) certified\n- CCD operations via FHIR\n\n**Reporting**\n- UDS (Uniform Data System) data collection and reporting — critical for FQHC compliance\n- Meaningful Use / Promoting Interoperability reporting\n- Patient-Centered Medical Home (PCMH) reporting\n- Practice operational reports: cycle times, appointments/encounters by hour, patient no-show rates\n\n**Scheduling**\n- Inherited from OpenEMR base platform (patient scheduling is a core OpenEMR feature)\n- Appointment tracking and reporting\n\n**Infrastructure**\n- Cloud-based (AWS-compatible), no on-premise hardware required\n- Nightly maintenance window (11:30 PM – 1:00 AM per login page)\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on the features described above and the OpenEMR foundation, ICE stores:\n\n- **Clinical records**: SOAP notes, encounter documentation across multiple specialties (primary care, pediatrics, women's health, psychiatry, therapy, MAT)\n- **Patient demographics**: Including SDOH data (certified for (a)(5) and (a)(15))\n- **Screening/assessment data**: Extensive screening tool results for SDOH, substance abuse, depression, fall risk, human trafficking, lead poisoning, vision, TB, smoking, dental, and Zika\n- **Medication data**: Prescriptions and e-prescribing records (historically via VeraDigm/Allscripts, now via NewCrop/SureScripts)\n- **Lab results**: From 20+ interfaced laboratories\n- **Radiology/imaging orders and results**\n- **Implantable device data**: Certified for (a)(14)\n- **Billing/claims data**: FQHC facility billing, fee-for-service claims, payment records, patient ledgers, payor aging\n- **Scheduling data**: Appointments, encounter tracking, no-show tracking\n- **Patient portal communications**: Secure messages between patients and providers\n- **Transitions of care documents**: CCDs, certified for (b)(1)\n- **Clinical quality measure data**: Certified for (c)(1)\n- **Chronic care management records**: Tracking, reporting, and billing data\n\n**What's unclear:**\n- The vendor website does not specifically mention dental EHR functionality, even though MyCHN provides dental services. It's unclear whether dental records are managed in ICE or in a separate system.\n- The vendor website does not mention pharmacy management, though MyCHN operates pharmacies.\n- The relationship between the core ICE product and the ancillary products (Elevations, SDOH Platform, Resource Manager, Data Warehouse, Remote Patient Monitoring) is not well-documented — it's unclear how much data flows between them or whether they share a database.\n\n---\n\n## Other Products in the CHN Tech Solutions Ecosystem\n\nThe vendor website lists several additional products beyond the core EHR:\n\n- **Elevations**: A community care coordination platform for \"whole-person care\" — closed-loop referrals, social care funding, community partnership management. Appears to be a separate product focused on social services coordination rather than clinical EHR.\n- **SDOH Platform**: Training-focused platform related to social determinants of health screening and data. Appears connected to the SDOH screening tools within ICE.\n- **Remote Patient Monitoring**: Listed as a product but no details available on the website.\n- **Resource Manager**: Listed as a product but no details available.\n- **Data Warehouse**: Listed as a product but no details available.\n\nThe degree of integration between these products and the core Integrated Care EHR is unclear from available sources.\n","numLines":128,"startLine":1,"totalLines":128}}}

.entry-header{min-height:200px;}\n\t\t\t\t Integrated Care EHR &#8211; Electronic Health Information Export \t\t\t \n\t\t \n\t \n \n \n\t \n\t\t \n\t\t\t\t\t\t \n\t\t\t\t \n\t \n\t\t\n \n\t\n 170.315(b)(10) Electronic Health Information Export \n Integrated Care EHR exports patient data using the C-CDA, which stands for Consolidated Clinical Document Architecture. It’s the most widely used format for health information exchange in the US today. \n C-CDA documents are generally represented in XML. They can include structured information like a medication list.&nbsp; \n C-CDA is generally read-only (although the information can be parsed and uploaded elsewhere with some effort). It’s a library of templates and can encompass information from a single point in time to an aggregation of one’s medical history. \n While the specification doesn&#8217;t define the transport mechanism for communication, the mechanisms for the communication of clinical documents are defined in a hierarchy of specifications. Integrated Care EHR, C-CDA export capabilities have been built upon the Technical Frameworks &#8211; IHE International . \n See the specific IHE Technical Framework Supplement for CDA Document Summary Sections, which is located here. \n Also see the official HL7.org Consolidated CDA Implementation Guide and standards . \n Volume 1 &amp; 2 of the above HL7.org documentation can also be viewed/downloaded from this website here (Vol1) and here (Vol2). \n \n\t \n \n\t\t\t \n\t\t\t\t\t \n\t\t\t \n \n\t \n\t \n\t \n\t\t \n\t \n\t\t\t\t \n\t\t\t \n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t \n\t\t\t\t\t\t\n \n\t \n\t\t &copy; .entry-header{min-height:200px;}\n\t\t\t\t Integrated Care EHR &#8211; Electronic Health Information Export \t\t\t \n\t\t \n\t \n \n \n\t \n\t\t \n\t\t\t\t\t\t \n\t\t\t\t \n\t \n\t\t\n \n\t\n 170.315(b)(10) Electronic Health Information Export \n Integrated Care EHR exports patient data using the C-CDA, which stands for Consolidated Clinical Document Architecture. It’s the most widely used format for health information exchange in the US today. \n C-CDA documents are generally represented in XML. They can include structured information like a medication list.&nbsp; \n C-CDA is generally read-only (although the information can be parsed and uploaded elsewhere with some effort). It’s a library of templates and can encompass information from a single point in time to an aggregation of one’s medical history. \n While the specification doesn&#8217;t define the transport mechanism for communication, the mechanisms for the communication of clinical documents are defined in a hierarchy of specifications. Integrated Care EHR, C-CDA export capabilities have been built upon the Technical Frameworks &#8211; IHE International . \n See the specific IHE Technical Framework Supplement for CDA Document Summary Sections, which is located here. \n Also see the official HL7.org Consolidated CDA Implementation Guide and standards . \n Volume 1 &amp; 2 of the above HL7.org documentation can also be viewed/downloaded from this website here (Vol1) and here (Vol2). \n \n\t \n \n\t\t\t \n\t\t\t\t\t \n\t\t\t \n \n\t \n\t \n\t \n\t\t \n\t \n\t\t\t\t \n\t\t\t \n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t \n\t\t\t\t\t\t\n \n\t \n\t\t &copy; See standard Integrated Care EHR API docs&nbsp; here . The Integrated Care EHR FHIR API conforms to the R4 specification and the US Core 3.1 Implementation Guide (IG). Prerequisite Enable the Standard FHIR service (/fhir/ endpoints) in Integrated Care EHR menu: Administration-&gt;Globals-&gt;Connectors-&gt;&#8221;Enable Integrated Care EHR Standard FHIR REST API&#8221; Using FHIR API Internally There are several ways to make API calls from an authorized session and maintain security: See the script at tests/api/InternalApiTest.php for examples of internal API use cases. Multisite Support Multisite is supported by including the site in the endpoint. When not using multisite or using the&nbsp; default &nbsp;multisite site, then a typical path would look like&nbsp; apis/default/fhir/patient . If you were using multisite and using a site called&nbsp; alternate , then the path would look like&nbsp; apis/alternate/fhir/patient . Authorization Integrated Care EHR uses OIDC compliant authorization for API. SSL is required and setting baseurl at Administration-&gt;Globals-&gt;Connectors-&gt;&#8217;Site Address (required for OAuth2 and FHIR)&#8217; is required. FHIR API Documentation The FHIR API is documented via Swagger, which can be viewed by going to the swagger directory in any Integrated Care EHR installation, under the fhir section. It can be accessed via this address, https://chntech.from-tx.com/ic_ehr_onc_public/swagger/ , or to view the Swagger documentation, click here . Standard FHIR endpoints Use https://chntech.from-tx.com/ic_ehr_onc_public/apis/default/fhir as base URI. Note that the default component can be changed to the name of the site when using the Integrated Care EHR multisite feature. Example: https://chntech.from-tx.com/ic_ehr_onc_public/apis/default/fhir/Patient returns a Patient&#8217;s bundle resource, etc The Bearer token is required for each Integrated Care EHR FHIR request (except for the Capability Statement), and is conveyed using an Authorization header. Note that the Bearer token is the access_token that is obtained in the Authorization section. When registering an API client to use with Swagger the following for the redirect url and launch url for the client. See this github issue for an example of a Smart App installation:&nbsp; #4148 Authorization Code Grant This is the recommended standard mechanism to obtain access/refresh tokens. This is done by using an OAuth2 client with provider url of&nbsp;oauth2/&lt;site&gt;; an example full path would be&nbsp;https://localhost:9300/oauth2/default. Standard OAUTH2 clients will retrieve the authorize URL from the FHIR /metadata endpoint, but if you are building your own client you can access the metadata or go directly to the&nbsp; https://localhost:9300/oauth2/default/authorize &nbsp;endpoint. Note that a refresh token is only supplied if the&nbsp;offline_access&nbsp;scope is provided when requesting authorization grant. You will need to pass the scopes you are requesting, the redirect_uri (must be one that was registered at the time of your client registration), and a state parameter which can be any value. Once authorization has finished the browser will be redirected to the URL specified in redirect_uri with an encrypted code value and the state value sent in the initial authorize request. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01CvdAw3BYTDXmj1YRugDkz8","type":"tool_result","content":"bsp; system/Group.$export system*.$bulkdata-status system/Binary.read Patient System export &#8211;&nbsp; system/Patient.$export system*.$bulkdata-status system/Binary.read 3rd Party SMART Apps Integrated Care EHR supports the ability for 3rd party apps who implement the&nbsp; SMART on FHIR App Launch Implementation Guide 1.1.0 &nbsp;context. 3rd party Apps using the confidential app profile are auto enabled if they are strictly a patient standalone app. A patient standalone app is one that only requests patient only scopes such as patient/*. A provider or system app (requesting permissions such as launch, user/*, system/*, etc) must be authorized by the Integrated Care EHR Server Installation Administrator. Access Tokens issued to 3rd party apps are only valid for one hour and must be renewed with a refresh token which is valid for up to three months. Refresh tokens are only issued if the offline_access scope is authorized by the Integrated Care EHR user authenticating with Integrated Care EHR through their 3rd party app. For a patient to have access to their patient data via a 3rd party app they must have api credentials generated by their clinician from the patient demographics page. A patient must not have opted out of 3rd party API access. Integrated Care EHR does NOT support wildcard scopes (patient/ . &nbsp;or patient/*.read). Scopes must be requested explicitly by an app at the time of registration. Integrated Care EHR does not support adding scopes from the initial registration. Revoking Clients, Users, Access Tokens, Refresh Tokens Revoking Clients You can disable a client completely which prevents their access tokens from being used in the system from the Admin -&gt; System -&gt; API Clients interface. Edit the client registered in your system you wish to disable and hit the Disable button. Revoking Users If you wish to revoke a user&#8217;s authorization for a particular client you will need to open up the API client from the Admin -&gt; System -&gt; API Clients interface. Once you are editing the client you will need to go to the Authenticated API Users section. From there you can find the user that is listed and hit the Revoke User button (Note this can be a lengthy list so use your browser&#8217;s search text functionality to find the user). Revoking Access Tokens You can revoke an access token two ways. One from the API Client edit screen, finding the client and then the access token&#8217;s identifier you wish to revoke. The second way is if you have the fully encoded access token using the API Client Tools screen. Go to Admin-&gt;System-&gt;API Clients and then click on the Token Tools button. Paste in the entire encoded token and then select Parse Token. Information about the token will be displayed including the authenticated user that authorized the token. Now select the Revoke Token button to revoke the token. A success message will be displayed when the revocation completes. You can parse the token again to see that the token has been revoked. Native Applications Interoperability requirements with Integrated Care EHR for Native Applications Native applications wishing to use the Integrated Care EHR FHIR API with refresh tokens MUST be capable of storing the refresh token in a secure manner similar to the requirements of storing a secret for confidential apps. Native applications must support either PKCE with a public app or use a confidential app. PKCE is still recommended to be used with confidential apps to prevent MITM attacks. Native applications must request the offline_scope in their initial API request in order to receive a refresh token Native application refresh tokens are valid for 3 months before they must be renewed. Native applications will receive a new refresh token every time they are renewed. Native applications can only communicate with Integrated Care EHR over a TLS secured channel in order to ensure the safe transmission of the refresh token. Native applications should use certificate pinning to mitigate any SSL MITM attack. Native applications must use the Authorization Code grant flow in order to receive a refresh token. It is recommended that native applications follow best practices for native client applications as outlined in RFC 8252 OAuth 2.0 for Native Apps. Carecoordination Summary of Care Docref Operation Overview Docref The $docref operation is used to request the server to generate a document based on the specified parameters. If no additional parameters are specified then a DocumentReference to the patient&#8217;s most current Clinical Summary of Care Document (CCD) is returned. The document itself is retrieved using the DocumentReference.content.attachment.url element. See&nbsp; http://hl7.org/fhir/us/core/OperationDefinition-docref.html &nbsp;for more details. Generate CCDA Tutorial to Generate CCDA (with Screenshots) Details Docref Requires /DocumentReference.$docref, /DocumentReference.read, and /Binary.read scopes Start and end date filter encounter related events for the following sections: History of Procedures Relevant DX Tests / LAB Data Functional Status Progress Notes Procedure Notes Laboratory Report Narrative Encounters Assessments Treatment Plan Goals Health Concerns Document Reason for Referral Mental Status The following sections have the entire medical record sent to ensure that medical professionals have medically necessary information to provide treatment of care: Demographics Allergies, Adverse Reactions, Alerts History of Medication Use Problem List Immunizations Social History Medical Equipment Vital Signs (shows the latest vitals recorded for the patient) CCD is generated on demand, saved off in patient&#8217;s documents record under the CCDA category. Returns a DocumentReference search bundle per the IG spec. XSL to view the document can be download at //interface/modules/zend_modules/public/xsl/cda.xsl Or xml file can be uploaded as a document into Integrated Care EHR to view in a human readable format Due to browser security restrictions XSL file must be in same directory as ccd document to view. If no start date is provided it will retrieve all records in the patient history up to the end date If no end date is provided it will retrieve all records connected to encounters starting from the start date If a records for a specific service date are desired, make the start date and end date the same day in YYYY-MM-DD format. Dates must be specified in least specificity to most specificity for wildcard like operations IE YYYY for the beginning of the year for start date or ending of the year for end date, YYYY-MM for the beginning of the month for start date and end of the month for end date, etc. For Developers FHIR endpoints are defined in the primary Rest_Routes file. A provider or system app (requesting permissions such as launch, user/*, system/*, etc) must be authorized by the Integrated Care EHR Server Installation Administrator. Access Tokens issued to 3rd party apps are only valid for one hour and must be renewed with a refresh token which is valid for up to three months. Refresh tokens are only issued if the offline_access scope is authorized by the Integrated Care EHR user authenticating with Integrated Care EHR through their 3rd party app. For a patient to have access to their patient data via a 3rd party app they must have api credentials generated by their clinician from the patient demographics page. A patient must not have opted out of 3rd party API access. Integrated Care EHR does NOT support wildcard scopes (patient/ . &nbsp;or patient/*.read). Scopes must be requested explicitly by an app at the time of registration. Integrated Care EHR does not support adding scopes from the initial registration. This is an on-demand document generation process, not a bulk database export.\n\n### Export Format\nThe export produces a single C-CDA XML document per patient. The CCD is generated dynamically and can be filtered by date range. The document is accessed through the FHIR DocumentReference resource and downloaded via the Binary endpoint.\n\n### CCD Sections Documented\nThe FHIR API page documents the following CCD sections, noting that date-filtered sections only include encounters within the specified date range:\n\n**Date-filterable sections:**\n- History of Procedures\n- Relevant DX Tests / LAB Data\n- Functional Status\n- Progress Notes\n- Procedure Notes\n- Laboratory Report Narrative\n- Encounters\n- Assessments\n- Treatment Plan\n- Goals\n- Health Concerns\n- Document Reason for Referral\n- Mental Status\n\n**Full medical record sections (always include complete history):**\n- Demographics\n- Allergies, Adverse Reactions, Alerts\n- History of Medication Use\n- Problem List\n- Immunizations\n- Social History\n- Medical Equipment\n- Vital Signs (latest recorded)\n\n### Additional API Capabilities\nThe site also documents:\n- **FHIR Bulk Data Export** supporting System, Group, and Patient export operations (this is the (g)(10) mechanism, not the (b)(10) export)\n- **OpenEMR native REST API** with endpoints covering: allergies, appointments, dental issues, documents, drugs, encounters, facilities, immunizations, insurance, medical problems, medications, messages, patients, practitioners, prescriptions, procedures, SOAP notes, surgeries, transactions, and vitals\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nThe (b)(10) EHI export is a **C-CDA CCD document**, which by its nature covers a defined set of clinical summary data. Based on the product research, here is the coverage analysis:\n\n**Clearly covered by the CCD export:**\n- Demographics (CCD section)\n- Allergies and adverse reactions (CCD section)\n- Medications / medication history (CCD section)\n- Problem list / diagnoses (CCD section)\n- Immunizations (CCD section)\n- Vital signs (CCD section, latest only)\n- Procedures (CCD section)\n- Lab results (CCD section)\n- Social history (CCD section)\n- Encounters (CCD section)\n- Goals and care plans (CCD sections)\n- Mental status (CCD section)\n- Medical equipment / implantable devices (CCD section)\n- Progress notes (CCD section)\n- Assessment and treatment plans (CCD sections)\n\n**Missing or not addressed by the CCD export:**\n- **Billing data** — FQHC facility billing, fee-for-service claims, payment records, patient ledgers, payor aging. The CCD format does not carry billing data, and no separate billing export mechanism is documented.\n- **Screening/assessment data** — The product stores extensive screening results (SDOH, substance abuse, depression, fall risk, human trafficking, lead poisoning, vision, TB, smoking, dental, Zika). While some of these might map to Observations in the CCD, the documentation does not describe how these screenings are represented in the export, and most would not fit standard CCD templates.\n- **Scheduling data** — Appointments, no-show tracking. Not included in CCD.\n- **Insurance/coverage data** — While the REST API has insurance scopes, the CCD does not carry insurance enrollment details.\n- **Patient portal communications** — Secure messages between patients and providers. Not included in CCD.\n- **E-prescribing records** — Detailed prescription transmission records (VeraDigm/NewCrop). The CCD includes medication lists but not prescription routing history.\n- **Chronic care management data** — Tracking, reporting, and billing data for CCM programs.\n- **Dental records** — The REST API includes `dental_issue` scopes, but the CCD does not have a dental section.\n- **Specialty clinical notes** — While progress notes are included, the CCD may not capture the full richness of specialty modules (psychiatry, therapy, MAT documentation, women's health).\n- **Documents and attachments** — External documents incorporated into the chart. The CCD is a generated summary, not an archive of all attached documents.\n\n**Ambiguous:**\n- **Lab/imaging reports** — The CCD includes lab results, but it's unclear whether all results from 20+ interfaced labs are fully represented, or whether imaging reports (from radiology interfaces) are included beyond diagnostic report narratives.\n- **Clinical quality measure data** — Not typically part of a CCD.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses C-CDA R2.1 (the standard clinical document format), generated via the FHIR `$docref` operation. This is a recognized, widely-used standard — but it is a **clinical summary format**, not a comprehensive data dump.\n\nThe fundamental problem: **a CCD is designed to summarize a patient's clinical state for transitions of care, not to export all electronic health information.** It covers a well-defined set of clinical domains but structurally cannot carry billing data, scheduling data, messaging history, or much of the specialty-specific data that this FQHC product stores.\n\nThe vendor has essentially pointed their (b)(10) certification at their (g)(10) FHIR API's `$docref` operation. The FHIR Bulk Data Export (also documented on the site) exports standard FHIR US Core resources, which would have similar coverage limitations. Neither mechanism addresses the full \"designated record set\" requirement of (b)(10).\n\nThe REST API scope list is actually more revealing than the CCD about what data the system stores. It includes scopes for dental issues, SOAP notes, surgeries, insurance, prescriptions, and transactions — data types that are not represented in the CCD export.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n- **Readability**: The documentation is clear and well-organized across several WordPress pages. The CCD generation tutorial with Swagger screenshots is practical and followable.\n- **Data dictionary**: There is **no data dictionary**. There are no field-level definitions, no schema documentation, no description of what data elements appear in each CCD section.\n- **Format specification**: The vendor relies entirely on the C-CDA standard (linking to the HL7 C-CDA IG PDFs hosted on their site) rather than documenting any vendor-specific customizations or constraints.\n- **Sample data**: No sample export files or example CCDs are provided.\n- **Developer usability**: A developer could follow the Swagger tutorial to generate a CCD, but would have no vendor-specific documentation to understand the structure or content of the resulting document. They would need to rely entirely on the generic C-CDA IG (913 pages for Volume 2 alone).\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Granularity**: The documentation lists CCD section names but provides no field-level detail. There is no mapping between EHR data fields and CCD elements.\n- **Value sets**: Not documented. The vendor relies on the C-CDA standard's value set bindings.\n- **Relationships**: Not applicable — the CCD is a flat document, not a relational export.\n- **Versioning**: No version history or change log for the documentation.\n\n### Overall Assessment\n\nCHN Tech Solutions has taken the common approach of equating their EHI export with their FHIR API's CCD generation capability. The documentation is functional for understanding *how* to generate a CCD, but it does not address the fundamental (b)(10) question: how does a patient (or their representative) obtain *all* of their electronic health information from this system?\n\nThe product is built on OpenEMR, an open-source EHR with a well-documented database schema. The REST API scope list reveals data domains (dental issues, insurance, transactions, SOAP notes, surgeries) that go well beyond what the CCD export covers. A more complete (b)(10) approach would either export from the underlying OpenEMR database or use the native REST API to retrieve all data types — but neither is documented as the EHI export mechanism.\n\nThis is a small vendor (possibly single-site deployment at an FQHC) that appears to have met the letter of certification by pointing to CCD generation, but the export covers perhaps 40-50% of the patient data domains the product stores. Billing, scheduling, dental, screening tools, and messaging are the most significant gaps.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://onc.chntechsolutions.com/integrated-care-ehr-electronic-health-information-export-cures/\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (static WordPress pages, though screenshots in the CCD tutorial require viewing)\n- Navigation complexity: one_click (EHI page is directly accessible; CCD instructions are one nav click away)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n- The Swagger API endpoint at `chntech.from-tx.com` could not be resolved via DNS, so the interactive API documentation could not be accessed.\n- The \"Standard API\" page and \"REST API Documentation\" page contain identical content.\n- The two C-CDA IG PDFs hosted on the site are standard HL7 documents, not vendor-specific — they provide no information about how this vendor's specific implementation generates CCDs.\n- No production FHIR endpoint is listed — only a testing endpoint that is not DNS-resolvable.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":270,"cache_read_input_tokens":98716,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":270},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"f4905977-2393-4e57-9d32-7d9b63232cc2","uuid":"c8ff8bfd-8830-48f2-9b90-5efa7bb41878"}
